{\rm d}_{v}G_{\geq 3}(c,v)[\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)]\end{pmatrix}\,.

System (
5.20
) also follows by (
5.9
) since, by
(
5.21
), (
5.10
) and

Q_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\top}=Q_{\hat{\jmath}}^{-1}
,

(5.23)

{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)=Q_{\hat{\jmath}}{\mathcal{A}}(v)Q_{\hat{\jmath}}^{-1}\,.

The components of
{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)

have different sizes in
\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v
and

\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v
.

Lemma 5.6
.

(Properties of
{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)
)

For any
{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
, for any
v\in V
,

(5.24)

\left(\begin{matrix}\|v\|^{2}&\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\\
\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}&\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\end{matrix}\right)\geq{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)\geq\left(\begin{matrix}\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v\|^{2}&-\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\\
-\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}&\delta\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\end{matrix}\right)

componentwise,
where
\delta\in(0,1]
is defined in (
5.4
).
For any
\hat{v}\in V
,

(5.25)

\displaystyle{\rm d}_{v}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)[\hat{v}]={\mathcal{O}}\left(\begin{matrix}\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}\hat{v}\|+\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}\hat{v}\|&\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}\hat{v}\|\\
\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}\hat{v}\|&\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}\hat{v}\|\end{matrix}\right)\,.

Proof.

By (
5.23
),
(
5.12
),

Q_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\top}=Q_{\hat{\jmath}}^{-1}
,
and recalling (
4.36
), (
4.37
),
we get

{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)=\sum_{{\hat{\jmath}^{\prime}}\in{\mathcal{D}}}\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}^{\prime}}v\|^{2}(Q_{\hat{\jmath}}\hat{\jmath}^{\prime})\otimes(Q_{\hat{\jmath}}\hat{\jmath}^{\prime})\,,\quad(Q_{\hat{\jmath}}\hat{\jmath}^{\prime})\otimes(Q_{\hat{\jmath}}\hat{\jmath}^{\prime})=\begin{pmatrix}(\hat{\jmath}^{\prime}\cdot\hat{\jmath})^{2}&(\hat{\jmath}^{\prime}\cdot\hat{\jmath})(\hat{\jmath}^{\prime}\cdot\hat{\jmath}^{\bot})\\
(\hat{\jmath}^{\prime}\cdot\hat{\jmath})(\hat{\jmath}^{\prime}\cdot\hat{\jmath}^{\bot})&(\hat{\jmath}^{\prime}\cdot\hat{\jmath}^{\bot})^{2}\end{pmatrix}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\|v\|^{2}\geq{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{11}(v)=\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v\|^{2}+\sum_{{\hat{\jmath}^{\prime}}\in{\mathcal{D}}\setminus[j]}(\hat{\jmath}^{\prime}\cdot\hat{\jmath})^{2}\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}^{\prime}}v\|^{2}\geq\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v\|^{2}

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\geq{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{21}(v)={\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{12}(v)=\sum_{{\hat{\jmath}^{\prime}}\in{\mathcal{D}}\setminus[j]}(\hat{\jmath}^{\prime}\cdot\hat{\jmath})(\hat{\jmath}^{\prime}\cdot\hat{\jmath}^{\bot})\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}^{\prime}}v\|^{2}\geq-\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\geq{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{22}(v)=\sum_{{\hat{\jmath}^{\prime}}\in{\mathcal{D}}\setminus[j]}(\hat{\jmath}^{\prime}\cdot\hat{\jmath}^{\bot})^{2}\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}^{\prime}}v\|^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:def b}}}{{\geq}}\delta\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\,.

Formula (
5.25
) follows directly
by differentiation.
∎

The lower bound (
5.24
) implies that,
for any
v\in B_{r}^{V}
satisfying
0<\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|\leq\sqrt{\delta/4}\,\|v\|
,

\det({\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v))\geq\det\left(\begin{matrix}\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v\|^{2}&-\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\\
-\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}&\delta\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\end{matrix}\right)\geq\frac{\delta}{2}\|v\|^{2}\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}>0\,,

and, using also the upper bound
(
5.24
),

(5.26)

{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)^{-1}=\frac{1}{\det({\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v))}\left(\begin{matrix}{\mathcal{A}}_{22}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)&-{\mathcal{A}}_{12}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)\\
-{\mathcal{A}}_{12}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)&{\mathcal{A}}_{11}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)\end{matrix}\right)\leq\frac{2}{\delta}\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{1}{\|v\|^{2}}&\frac{1}{\|v\|^{2}}\\
\frac{1}{\|v\|^{2}}&\frac{1}{\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}}\end{matrix}\right)\,.

System (
5.20
) is thus equivalent,
for any
v\neq 0
, to find a
fixed point of

c^{\hat{\jmath}}=c^{\hat{\jmath}}_{*}-{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)^{-1}{\mathtt{G}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{\geq 3}(c^{\hat{\jmath}},v)=:{\mathcal{G}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(c^{\hat{\jmath}},v)\,.

The next key estimates
enable to compensate the singularity
of
{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)^{-1}

as
\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\to 0
.
They follow by general properties of
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-invariant functions, proved in Appendix
A
.

Lemma 5.7
.

For any
v\in B_{r}^{V}
and any
c^{\hat{\jmath}}\in B_{r}(c_{*})
, it results

(5.27)

\displaystyle{\mathtt{G}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{\geq 3}(c^{\hat{\jmath}},v)={\mathcal{O}}\begin{pmatrix}\|v\|^{3}\\
\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\|v\|\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{
uniformly in}\ c^{\hat{\jmath}}\,,

(5.28)

\displaystyle\partial_{c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}}{\mathtt{G}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{\geq 3}(c^{\hat{\jmath}},v)={\mathcal{O}}\begin{pmatrix}\|v\|^{3}\\
\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\|v\|\end{pmatrix}\,,\ \ \partial_{c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}}{\mathtt{G}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{\geq 3}(c^{\hat{\jmath}},v)={\mathcal{O}}\begin{pmatrix}\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\|v\|\\
\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\|v\|\end{pmatrix}\,,

where

\partial_{c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}}=\hat{\jmath}\cdot\partial_{c}
and
\partial_{c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}}=\hat{\jmath}^{\bot}\cdot\partial_{c}

denote directional derivatives.

Proof.

Using (
5.11
)
the component
{\mathtt{G}}_{\geq 3}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(c^{\hat{\jmath}},v)

in (
5.22
)
satisfies
{\mathtt{G}}_{\geq 3}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(c^{\hat{\jmath}},v)={\mathcal{O}}(\|v\|^{3})
. Moreover by (
4.39
), if
v\in V_{\hat{\jmath}}
then

\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)=0
,
and therefore

(5.29)

{\mathtt{G}}_{\geq 3}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}(c^{\hat{\jmath}},v)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{Def tg}}}{{=}}{\rm d}_{v}G_{\geq 3}(c,v)[\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)]=0\,,\quad\ \ \forall v\in B_{r}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}}\,.

By (
5.11
)
we also have

{\mathtt{G}}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{\geq 3}(c^{\hat{\jmath}},v)=\hat{\jmath}^{\bot}\cdot{\mathtt{G}}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{\geq 3}(c^{\hat{\jmath}},v)={\mathcal{O}}(\|v\|^{3})
and Lemma
A.2
-
(ii)
implies the second estimate in (
5.27
).
The first estimates in (
5.28
) follow by (
5.27
).
By Proposition
4.12

we have

\partial_{c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}}{\mathtt{G}}_{\geq 3}^{\hat{\jmath}}(c^{\hat{\jmath}},v)=0
for any

v\in B_{r}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}}
.
Lemma
A.2
-
(ii)
applied to
\partial_{c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}}{\mathtt{G}}_{\geq 3}^{\hat{\jmath}}(c^{\hat{\jmath}},v)
implies the second estimates in (
5.28
).
∎

From (
5.17
), (
5.26
), (
5.27
),
(
5.28
) we deduce that for
r^{\prime}\in(0,r)
sufficiently small, the map

{\mathcal{G}}^{\hat{\jmath}}:B_{r}(c_{*})\times[{\mathcal{N}}_{\hat{\jmath}}(r^{\prime})\setminus V_{\hat{\jmath}}]\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\,,\quad{\mathcal{G}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(c^{\hat{\jmath}},v):=c^{\hat{\jmath}}_{*}-{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)^{-1}{\mathtt{G}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{\geq 3}(c^{\hat{\jmath}},v)\,,

satisfies

(5.30)

|{\mathcal{G}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(c^{\hat{\jmath}},v)-c^{\hat{\jmath}}_{*}|\lesssim\|v\|\,,\quad|\partial_{c^{\hat{\jmath}}}{\mathcal{G}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(c^{\hat{\jmath}},v)|\lesssim\|v\|\,,

uniformly in
c
.
We deduce that if
r^{\prime}
is sufficiently small
{\mathcal{G}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(\cdot,v)
is a contraction in
B_{r}(c_{*})
and hence there exists a unique fixed point

c^{\hat{\jmath}}=c_{{3d}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)
of
{\mathcal{G}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(c^{\hat{\jmath}},v)=c^{\hat{\jmath}}
.
Since
{\mathcal{G}}^{\hat{\jmath}}
is analytic the function
v\mapsto c^{\hat{\jmath}}_{{3d}}(v)
is analytic.
Since
c_{{3d}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)
is a fixed point of
{\mathcal{G}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(\cdot,v)
from (
5.30
) we deduce

(5.31)

c_{{3d}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)=c^{\hat{\jmath}}_{*}+{\mathcal{O}}(\|v\|)\,,\quad\forall v\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\hat{\jmath}}(r^{\prime})\setminus V_{\hat{\jmath}}\,.

Thus (
5.5
) holds on
B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\setminus{\bf V}^{2d}
.

Lemma 5.8
.

(
5.7
) hold.

Proof.

Differentiating
{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)(c_{{3d}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)-c^{\hat{\jmath}}_{*})+{\mathtt{G}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{\geq 3}(c_{{3d}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v),v)=0
, for any
v\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\hat{\jmath}}(r^{\prime})\setminus V_{\hat{\jmath}}
we have

(5.32)

\big({\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)+\partial_{c^{\hat{\jmath}}}{\mathtt{G}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{\geq 3}(c_{{3d}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v),v)\big){\rm d}_{v}c_{{3d}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(c)[\hat{v}]=-{\rm d}_{v}{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)[\hat{v}](c_{{3d}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)-c_{*}^{\hat{\jmath}})-{\rm d}_{v}{\mathtt{G}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{\geq 3}(c_{{3d}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v),v)[\hat{v}]\,.

By (
5.24
), the matrix

(5.33)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)+\partial_{c^{\hat{\jmath}}}{\mathtt{G}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{\geq 3}(c^{\hat{\jmath}},v)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{Partial c1 g}}}{{=}}{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)+{\mathcal{O}}\left(\begin{matrix}\|v\|^{3}&\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\|v\|\\
\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\|v\|&\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\|v\|\end{matrix}\right)\,,\ v\in B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}

is invertible for any
v\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\hat{\jmath}}(r^{\prime}){\setminus V_{\hat{\jmath}}}
provided
r^{\prime}\in(0,r)
is sufficiently small, and

(5.34)

\big({\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)+\partial_{c^{\hat{\jmath}}}{\mathtt{G}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{\geq 3}(c^{\hat{\jmath}},v)\big)^{-1}={\mathcal{O}}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{\|v\|^{2}}&\frac{1}{\|v\|^{2}}\\
\frac{1}{\|v\|^{2}}&\frac{1}{\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}}\end{pmatrix}\,.

Using (
5.29
), (
5.11
) and Lemma
A.2
we deduce

(5.35)

{\rm d}_{v}{\mathtt{G}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{\geq 3}(c^{\hat{\jmath}},v)[\hat{v}]={\mathcal{O}}\begin{pmatrix}\|v\|^{2}\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}\hat{v}\|+\|v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}\hat{v}\|\\
\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}\hat{v}\|+\|v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}\hat{v}\|\end{pmatrix}\,,\ v\in B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\,.

Hence, by (
5.25
), (
5.31
) we obtain

(5.36)

-{\rm d}_{v}{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)[\hat{v}](c^{\hat{\jmath}}_{{3d}}(v)-c_{*}^{\hat{\jmath}})-{\rm d}_{v}{\mathtt{G}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{\geq 3}(c^{\hat{\jmath}}_{{3d}},v)[\hat{v}]={\mathcal{O}}\begin{pmatrix}\|v\|^{2}\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}\hat{v}\|+\|v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}\hat{v}\|\\
\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}\hat{v}\|+\|v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}\hat{v}\|\end{pmatrix}.

From (
5.32
), (
5.34
) and (
5.36
) we deduce (
5.7
).
∎

5.3
Construction of
c(v)
on
{\bf V}^{{2d}}

For any
{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}

and
v\in V_{\hat{\jmath}}
system (
5.1
) amounts to

{\rm d}_{v}\Phi(c,v)\Big[\hat{\jmath}\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)+\hat{\jmath}^{\bot}\underbrace{\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)}_{=0\,\text{by}\eqref{pibot}}\Big]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{Phi is independent on c2Q if v is in V2dj}}}{{=}}{\rm d}_{v}\Phi(c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}\hat{\jmath}+c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{*}\hat{\jmath}^{\bot},v)\Big[\hat{\jmath}\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)\Big]=0

and we are reduced to look for a scalar function

c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)

solving the equation

(5.37)

{\rm d}_{v}\Phi(c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}\hat{\jmath}+c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{*}\hat{\jmath}^{\bot},v)[\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)]=0\,,\quad\forall v\in V_{\hat{\jmath}}\cap B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\setminus\{0\}\,.

Remark 5.9
.

For any
v\in B_{r}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}}
, if
c\in B_{r}^{V}
solves (
5.1
) then
c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}=c\cdot\hat{\jmath}
solves (
5.37
).
Viceversa if
c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}
solves (
5.37
) then
c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}\hat{\jmath}+k\hat{\jmath}^{\bot}
solves (
5.1
) for any
k\in{\mathbb{R}}
such that
c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}\hat{\jmath}+k\hat{\jmath}^{\bot}\in B_{r}^{V}
.

In view of (
5.19
),
(
5.20
)-(
5.22
)
and (
4.39
), the equation (
5.37
) is equivalent to

(5.38)

{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{11}(v)(c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}-c_{*}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}})+{\mathtt{G}}^{\parallel\hat{\jmath}}_{\geq 3}(c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}},c_{*}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}},v)=0

where
{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{11}(v)
is defined in
(
5.21
)
and

{\mathtt{G}}^{\parallel\hat{\jmath}}_{\geq 3}(c^{\hat{\jmath}},v)
in (
5.22
).

Lemma 5.10
.

(Speed
c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{{2d}}(v)
)

There exist
r^{\prime}\in(0,r)
and, for any
{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
, a
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
-invariant analytic function

c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{{2d}}:(V_{\hat{\jmath}}\cap B_{r^{\prime}}^{V})\setminus\{0\}\to\big(c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{*}-r,c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{*}+r\big)
,
v\mapsto c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{{2d}}(v)
,
which is the unique solution of (
5.37
), satisfying

(5.39)

|c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{{2d}}(v)-c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{*}|\lesssim\|v\|\,,\quad|{\rm d}_{v}c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{{2d}}(v)[\hat{v}]|\lesssim\|\hat{v}\|\,,\quad\forall v\in V_{\hat{\jmath}}\cap B_{r^{\prime}}^{V},\,\hat{v}\in V_{\hat{\jmath}}\,.

Proof.

To simplify notation we set

{\mathtt{G}}_{{2d}}(c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}},v):={\mathtt{G}}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{\geq 3}(c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}},c_{*}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}},v)
.
In view of (
5.24
) the equation (
5.38
) is equivalent, for any
v\neq 0
, to find a fixed point of

{\mathcal{G}}_{{2d}}:\big(c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{*}-r,c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{*}+r\big)\times B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\to{\mathbb{R}}\,,\quad{\mathcal{G}}_{{2d}}(c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}},v):=c_{*}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}+({\mathcal{A}}_{11}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v))^{-1}{\mathtt{G}}_{{2d}}(c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}},v)\,,

for some
r^{\prime}\in(0,r)
.
Since
{\mathtt{G}}_{{2d}}(c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}},v)={\mathcal{O}}(\|v\|^{3})
uniformly in
c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}
, for any

c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}\in\big(c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{*}-r,c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{*}+r\big)
and any
v\in B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\setminus\{0\}
, we have

(5.40)

|{\mathcal{G}}_{{2d}}(c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}},v)|\lesssim\|v\|\,,\qquad|\partial_{c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}}{\mathcal{G}}_{{2d}}(c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}},v)|\lesssim\|v\|\,.

Therefore, for
r^{\prime}\in(0,r)
small
enough, the map

{\mathcal{G}}_{{2d}}(\cdot,v):\big(c_{*}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}-r,c_{*}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}+r\big)\to\big(c_{*}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}-r,c_{*}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}+r\big)
is a contraction and
for any
v\in B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}
there exists a unique fixed point
c_{{2d}}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)
of
{\mathcal{G}}_{{2d}}(\cdot,v)
.
The function
v\mapsto c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{{2d}}(v)
is analytic by applying the implicit function theorem to the analytic function

{\mathcal{G}}_{{2d}}(c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}},v)
.
The first bound in (
5.39
) follows from
{\mathcal{G}}_{{2d}}(c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{{2d}}(v),v)=c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{{2d}}(v)
and (
5.40
).
Taking the differential of (
5.38
) which is satisfied identically for
c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}=c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{{2d}}(v)
we have

(5.41)

\big({\mathcal{A}}_{11}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)+\partial_{c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}}{\mathtt{G}}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{\geq 3}(c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}},c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{*},v)\big){\rm d}_{v}c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)[\hat{v}]=-{\rm d}_{v}{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{11}(v)[\hat{v}](c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}-c_{*}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}})-{\rm d}_{v}{\mathtt{G}}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{\geq 3}(c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}},c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{*},v)[\hat{v}]\,.

Thus using (
5.24
), (
5.33
),
\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v=v
, (
5.25
) ,(
5.35
) and the first bound in (
5.39
) we obtain the estimate for the derivative in (
5.39
).
∎

Definition of
c(v)
.

We define the function
c(v)
in Proposition
5.1
as

(5.42)

c:B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\to B_{r}(c_{*})\,,\quad c(v):=\begin{cases}c_{{3d}}(v)&\text{if}\ v\in B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}\\
c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{{2d}}(v)\hat{\jmath}+c_{*}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}\hat{\jmath}^{\bot}&\text{if}\ v\in(B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}})\setminus\{0\}\,,\quad{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}\,,\\
c_{*}&\text{if}\ v=0\,,\end{cases}

where
c_{{3d}}:B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is the analytic function
defined in Propositions
5.2
,
5.5
and

c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{{2d}}:B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is the analytic function
defined in Lemma
5.10
.
In view of Propositions
5.2
and
5.5
and
(
5.39
),
the function
c(v)
satisfies (
5.5
) and so it is continuous at
v=0
.

5.4
Regularity properties

To complete the proof of
Proposition

5.1
we have to prove the
regularity properties in
(
5.8
).

Lemma 5.11
.

(Continuity
of
c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)
)

For any
{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
, the parallel component
c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)=\hat{\jmath}\cdot c(v)
of the speed
c(v)
defined in (
5.42
) is continuous on the set

B_{r^{\prime}}^{\hat{\jmath}}:=(B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}})\cup(B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}})
.

Proof.

We have just to prove the continuity
of
c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)

at any
\bar{v}\in B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}}
,
\bar{v}\neq 0
.
Let
\{v_{i}\}_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}
be a sequence in
B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}
converging to
\bar{v}
.
Since
c(v_{i})
is bounded (cf. (
5.5
)), for any subsequence
v_{i_{m}}
there is
v_{{i_{m}}_{\ell}}
such that

c(v_{{i_{m}}_{\ell}})\xrightarrow[\ell\to+\infty]{}\bar{c}
and
\bar{c}\in\overline{B_{r^{\prime}}(c_{*})}\subset B_{r}(c_{*})
for
r^{\prime}\in(0,r)
sufficiently small.
Since
c(v_{i})
solves (
5.1
) (equiv. (
5.19
)), by continuity, we deduce that

{\rm d}_{v}\Phi(\bar{c},\bar{v})[\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(\bar{v})]=0
,
and hence
\bar{c}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}
solves
(
5.37
).
By uniqueness,
Lemma
5.10
implies that
\bar{c}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}=c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{{2d}}(\bar{v})
and thus
\lim_{i\to+\infty}c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v_{i})=c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{{2d}}(\bar{v})=c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(\bar{v})
. This proves that
c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)
is continuous.
∎

Actually
c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)
is also
C^{1}

on
B_{r^{\prime}}^{\hat{\jmath}}\setminus\{0\}
as we show in Lemma
5.13
below.

Lemma 5.12
.

Let
{\mathtt{E}}:B_{r}(c_{*})\times B_{r}^{V}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be an analytic
function such that, for any

{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
,

(5.43)

\partial_{c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}}{\mathtt{E}}(c,v)=0\quad\forall v\in V_{\hat{\jmath}}\cap B_{r}^{V},\ c\in B_{r}(c_{*})\,.

Then the composed functions

v\mapsto{\mathtt{E}}(c(v),v)
and
v\mapsto(\nabla_{v}{\mathtt{E}})(c(v),v)
, with
c(v)
defined in
(
5.42
),
are continuous on
B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}
, analytic on
B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}
and
restricted to each
(B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}})\setminus\{0\}
for any
{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
.

Proof.

Both the functions
v\mapsto{\mathtt{E}}(c(v),v)
and
v\mapsto(\nabla_{v}{\mathtt{E}})(c(v),v)
are analytic on
B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}
and restricted to
(B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}})\setminus\{0\}
for any
{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
by the analyticity property of the function
c(v)
.
Let us prove the continuity of
v\mapsto{\mathtt{E}}(c(v),v)
.
Since the function
c(v)
is continuous at
v=0
(cf. (
5.5
)) we only have to verify it is continuous
at
\bar{v}\in B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}}
,
\bar{v}\neq 0
.
Let
\{v_{i}\}_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}
be a sequence in
B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}
converging to
\bar{v}
. Since
c(v_{i})

is bounded (cf. (
5.5
)),
for any subsequence
v_{i_{m}}
there exists a subsequence
v_{i_{m_{\ell}}}
and
\bar{c}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}\in(c_{*}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}-r,c_{*}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}+r)
such that

c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v_{i_{m_{\ell}}})\xrightarrow[\ell\to+\infty]{}\bar{c}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}
.
Hence,
using also the continuity
of
c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)
proved in Lemma
5.11
, we deduce

c(v_{i_{m_{\ell}}})\to\bar{c}:=c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{{2d}}(\bar{v})\hat{\jmath}+\bar{c}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}\hat{\jmath}^{\bot}
and

{\mathtt{E}}(c(v_{i_{m_{\ell}}}),v_{i_{m_{\ell}}})\xrightarrow[\ell\to+\infty]{}{\mathtt{E}}(\bar{c},\bar{v})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{laderab}}}{{=}}{\mathtt{E}}(c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{{2d}}(\bar{v})\hat{\jmath}+c_{*}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}\hat{\jmath}^{\bot},\bar{v})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{c defined on the ball}}}{{=}}{\mathtt{E}}(c(\bar{v}),\bar{v})\,.

Hence
v\mapsto{\mathtt{E}}(c(v),v)
is continuous.
The
continuity of
(\nabla_{v}{\mathtt{E}})(c(v),v)
is similar.
∎

Lemma 5.13
.

(
C^{1}
regularity of
c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)
)

For any
{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
,
the parallel component
c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)
of the speed
c(v)
in (
5.42
) is of class
C^{1}
on
B_{r^{\prime}}^{\hat{\jmath}}\setminus\{0\}
.

Proof.

We have just to prove that

c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)

is
C^{1}

at any
\bar{v}\in B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}}
,
\bar{v}\neq 0
.
We show that for any direction
\hat{v}\in V
the derivative
{\rm d}_{v}c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)[\hat{v}]
on
B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}
admits a continuous extension to
B_{r^{\prime}}^{\hat{\jmath}}\setminus\{0\}
.
For any
\hat{v}\in V
, let
\ell:B_{r^{\prime}}^{\hat{\jmath}}\setminus\{0\}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be the function

\ell(v):={\rm d}_{v}c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)[\hat{v}]\quad\forall\ v\in B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\setminus{\bf V}^{2d}\,,

and for any
v\in(B_{r^{\prime}}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}})\setminus\{0\}
be the unique solution of the equation (cf. (
5.41
))

(5.44)

\big({\mathcal{A}}_{11}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)+\partial_{c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}}{\mathtt{G}}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{\geq 3}(c^{\hat{\jmath}}(v),v)\big)\ell(v)=-{\rm d}_{v}{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{11}(v)[\hat{v}](c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)-c_{*}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}})-{\rm d}_{v}{\mathtt{G}}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{\geq 3}(c^{\hat{\jmath}}(v),v)[\hat{v}]\,.

In view of (
5.24
) and (
5.33
) the solution of (
5.44
) is unique.
We now prove that
\ell(v)
is continuous
at any
\bar{v}\in(B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}})\setminus\{0\}
.
Let
\{v_{i}\}_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}
be a sequence in
B_{r^{\prime}}^{\hat{\jmath}}\setminus\{0\}
converging to
\bar{v}
.
For any subsequence
v_{i_{m}}
there exists a subsequence
v_{i_{m_{\ell}}}
which is entirely contained in
(B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}})\setminus\{0\}
or in
B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\setminus{\bf V}^{2d}
.
In the first case taking the limit in (
5.44
) by uniqueness we obtain that
\ell(v_{i_{m_{\ell}}})\to\ell(\bar{v})
.
Hence assume that
v_{i_{m_{\ell}}}\in B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\setminus{\bf V}^{2d}
.
In view of (
5.7
) we have that
\ell(v_{i_{m_{\ell}}})={\rm d}_{v}c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v_{i_{m_{\ell}}})[\hat{v}]
is bounded.
Hence there exists
\bar{\mu}\in{\mathbb{R}}
such that

\ell(v_{i_{m_{\ell}}})={\rm d}_{v}c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v_{i_{m_{\ell}}})[\hat{v}]\to\bar{\mu}
up to subsequence.
In view of (
5.32
) we have

(5.45)

\displaystyle\big({\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{11}(v)+\partial_{c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}}{\mathtt{G}}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{\geq 3}(c^{\hat{\jmath}}(v),v)\big)\ell(v)+\underbrace{\big({\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{12}(v)+\partial_{c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}}{\mathtt{G}}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{\geq 3}(c^{\hat{\jmath}}(v),v)\big){\rm d}_{v}c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}(c)[\hat{v}]}_{=:\boldsymbol{(I_{\ell})}}

\displaystyle=-{\rm d}_{v}{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{11}(v)[\hat{v}](c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)-c_{*}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}})-\underbrace{{\rm d}_{v}{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}_{12}(v)[\hat{v}](c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)-c_{*}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}})}_{=:\boldsymbol{(II_{\ell})}}-{\rm d}_{v}{\mathtt{G}}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{\geq 3}(c^{\hat{\jmath}}(v),v)[\hat{v}]

for
v=v_{i_{m_{\ell}}}
.
Using (
5.24
), (
5.33
) and (
5.7
) we deduce
\boldsymbol{(I_{\ell})}\xrightarrow[\ell\to+\infty]{}0
and using (
5.25
), (
5.5
) that
\boldsymbol{(II_{\ell})}\xrightarrow[\ell\to+\infty]{}0
.
The other terms converge in view of Lemmata
5.11
and
5.12
and since
\partial_{c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}}{\mathtt{G}}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{\geq 3}(c,v)=0
for
v\in B_{r}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}}
.
Passing to the limit for
\ell\to+\infty
in (
5.45
) we deduce that
\bar{\mu}
solves (
5.44
) at
\bar{v}
.
Since the solution to (
5.44
) is unique we deduce
\ell(\bar{v})=\bar{\mu}
and hence
\ell(v_{i})={\rm d}_{v}c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v_{i})[\hat{v}]
converges to
\ell(\bar{v})
.
We have proved that
\ell(v)
is a continuous extension of
{\rm d}_{v}c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)[\hat{v}]
.
∎

We conclude with another lemma which will be used in Lemmata
6.1

and
8.2
.

Lemma 5.14
.

Let
{\mathtt{E}}:B_{r}(c_{*})\times B_{r}^{V}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be an analytic

{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-invariant
function such that

(5.46)

{\mathtt{E}}(c,0)=0\,,\quad\partial_{c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}}{\mathtt{E}}(c,v)=0\,,\ \forall v\in B_{r}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}}\,,\ c\in B_{r}(c_{*})\,,\ {\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}\,.

Then the composed function
v\mapsto{\mathtt{E}}(c(v),v)
, with
c(v)
defined in
(
5.42
), is
C^{1}(B_{r^{\prime}}^{V})
.

Proof.

Note that, since
{\mathtt{E}}(c,\cdot)
is
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-invariant, Lemma
A.1

implies that

(5.47)

{\rm d}_{v}{\mathtt{E}}(c,0)=0\quad\forall c\in B_{r}(c_{*})\,.

By Lemma
5.12
the
composed function
E(v):={\mathtt{E}}(c(v),v)
is analytic on
B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}
and
continuous on
B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}

and we
have just to prove that
E
is
C^{1}
on each

V_{\hat{\jmath}}\cap B_{r^{\prime}}
and at
v=0
.
Note that

B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}=\cup_{\hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}}{\mathcal{F}}_{r^{\prime}}\cup{\mathcal{N}}_{\hat{\jmath}}(r^{\prime})
(with
{\mathcal{F}}_{r^{\prime}}
and
{\mathcal{N}}_{\hat{\jmath}}(r^{\prime})
defined in (
5.2
) and (
5.3
))
and we
show that for any direction
\hat{v}\in V
, for any
\hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}
the restriction of
{\rm d}_{v}E(v)[\hat{v}]
to
{\mathcal{F}}_{r^{\prime}}\cup{\mathcal{N}}_{\hat{\jmath}}(r^{\prime})\setminus V_{\hat{\jmath}}
admits a continuous extension to
{\mathcal{F}}_{r^{\prime}}\cup{\mathcal{N}}_{\hat{\jmath}}(r^{\prime})
which is equal to
0
at
v=0
.
For any
{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
, and
v\in B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\setminus{\bf V}^{2d}
, we have

{\rm d}_{v}E(v)[\hat{v}]=\underbrace{({\rm d}_{v}{\mathtt{E}})(c(v),v)[\hat{v}]}_{=:\boldsymbol{(I)}}+\underbrace{(\partial_{c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}}{\mathtt{E}})(c(v),v){\rm d}_{v}c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)[\hat{v}]}_{=:\boldsymbol{(II)}}+\underbrace{(\partial_{c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}}{\mathtt{E}})(c(v),v){\rm d}_{v}c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)[\hat{v}]}_{\boldsymbol{(III)}}\,.

By Lemma
5.12
the term
\boldsymbol{(I)}
admits a continuous extension on
B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}
which is equal to
0
at
v=0
by (
5.46
).
Similarly Lemma
5.12
implies that
(\partial_{c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}}{\mathtt{E}})(c(v),v)
is continuous on
B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}
.
Since
{\rm d}_{v}c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)
is continuous on
B_{r^{\prime}}^{\hat{\jmath}}\setminus\{0\}
(by Lemma
5.13
) the term
\boldsymbol{(II)}
extends with continuity to
B_{r^{\prime}}^{\hat{\jmath}}\setminus\{0\}
.
Moreover,
{\rm d}_{v}c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)[\hat{v}]
is uniformly bounded on
{\mathcal{F}}_{r^{\prime}}\cup{\mathcal{N}}_{\hat{\jmath}}(r^{\prime})\setminus\{0\}

(by the first inequality in (
5.7
) and (
5.6
))
and
(\partial_{c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}}{\mathtt{E}})(c,v)={\mathcal{O}}(\|v\|^{2})
(which follows by (
5.46
) and (
5.47
)) we deduce that

|\boldsymbol{(II)}_{|{\mathcal{F}}_{r^{\prime}}\cup{\mathcal{N}}_{\hat{\jmath}}(r^{\prime})\setminus\{0\}}|\lesssim\|v\|^{2}\|\hat{v}\|

and so
\boldsymbol{(II)}
further extends with continuity at
0
.
Lemma

A.2
-
(ii)
implies that
(\partial_{c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}}{\mathtt{E}})(c(v),v)={\mathcal{O}}(\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2})
and using also the second inequality in (
5.7
) and (
5.6
) we deduce that

|\boldsymbol{(III)}_{|{\mathcal{F}}_{r^{\prime}}\cup{\mathcal{N}}_{\hat{\jmath}}(r^{\prime})\setminus V_{\hat{\jmath}}}|\lesssim\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|\|v\|\|\hat{v}\|

and hence
\boldsymbol{(III)}
extends with continuity to
{\mathcal{F}}_{r^{\prime}}\cup{\mathcal{N}}_{\hat{\jmath}}(r^{\prime})
.
∎

6
Straightening of the reduced momentum

Let
I(v)
be the reduced momentum

(6.1)

I:B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\,,\qquad I(v):={\mathtt{I}}(c(v),v)={\mathcal{I}}(v+w(c(v),v))\,,

where
{\mathtt{I}}(c,v)
is defined in (
4.45
) and
c(v)
in Proposition

5.1
.

Lemma 6.1
.

The reduced momentum
I
in
(
6.1
)
is
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
-invariant,
C^{1}(B_{r^{\prime}}^{V})
, analytic in

B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}
and analytic restricted to each
(B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}})\setminus\{0\}
for any
{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
.

Proof.

Since
{\mathtt{I}}(c,v)
and
c(v)
are
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
-invariant in
v
we deduce that
I(v)
is
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
-invariant.
The function
{\mathtt{I}}(c,v)=\partial_{c}\Phi(c,v)
in (
4.45
)-(
4.46
) satisfies the assumptions of Lemma
5.14
, by
Proposition
4.12
, and thus

I
in
C^{1}(B_{r^{\prime}}^{V})
. The other claimed analyticity properties of
I(v)
directly follow
by those of the speed function
c(v)
in Proposition
5.1
.
∎

The main result of this section is
the following “Morse type” result: the reduced
momentum
I(v)
can be locally
rectified, close to
0
,
into the purely quadratic momentum
{\mathcal{I}}(v)
.

Theorem 6.2
.

(Straightening)

There exist open neighborhoods

U_{1},U_{2}
of
0
in
V
with
U_{1},U_{2}\subset B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}
, and a
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-equivariant homeomorphism
\zeta:U_{1}\to U_{2}

satisfying
\zeta(0)=0
, such that

(6.2)

I\circ\zeta(v)={\mathcal{I}}(v)\,,\quad\forall v\in U_{1}\,,

where
I(v)
is defined in (
6.1
) and
{\mathcal{I}}(v)
in (
4.34
).
Furthermore

(6.3)

\displaystyle\zeta_{|U_{1}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}}:U_{1}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}\to U_{2}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}\quad\text{ is a diffeomorphism}\,,

(6.4)

\displaystyle\zeta_{|(U_{1}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}})\setminus\{0\}}:(U_{1}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}})\setminus\{0\}\to(U_{2}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}})\setminus\{0\}\,\ \forall{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}\,,\ \text{ is a diffeomorphism}\,.

Remark 6.3
.

Actually
U_{2}\subset B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}
where
r^{\prime\prime}\in(0,r^{\prime})
is fixed in Lemma
6.6
(cf. (
6.36
)).

Theorem
6.2
is proved in Section
6.2

using estimates proved in the
next section.

6.1
The reduced momentum
I(v)
and the matrix
A(v)

For any
{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
, we set

(6.5)

I^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)=\begin{pmatrix}I^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)\\
I^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)\end{pmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:def yj}}}{{:=}}Q_{\hat{\jmath}}I(v)\,,\quad{\mathcal{I}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)=\begin{pmatrix}{\mathcal{I}}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)\\
{\mathcal{I}}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)\end{pmatrix}=Q_{\hat{\jmath}}{\mathcal{I}}(v)\,.

The reduced momentum
I(v)
is a perturbation of the quadratic momentum
{\mathcal{I}}(v)
in (
4.34
).

Lemma 6.4
.

For any
{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
and any
v\in B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}

(6.6)

\displaystyle I^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)={\mathcal{I}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)+{\mathcal{O}}\begin{pmatrix}\|v\|^{3}\\
\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\|v\|\end{pmatrix}\,,

(6.7)

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}_{v}I^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)[\hat{v}]={\rm d}_{v}{\mathcal{I}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)[\hat{v}]+{\mathcal{O}}(\|v\|^{2}\|\hat{v}\|)\,,\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\qquad\ \ \,\forall v\in{\mathcal{F}}_{r^{\prime}}\ \text{cf.}\ \eqref{defFr}\,,

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}_{v}I^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)[\hat{v}]={\rm d}_{v}{\mathcal{I}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)[\hat{v}]+{\mathcal{O}}\begin{pmatrix}\|v\|(\|v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}\hat{v}\|+\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}\hat{v}\|)\\
\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|(\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}\hat{v}\|+\|v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}\hat{v}\|)\end{pmatrix}\ \forall v\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\hat{\jmath}}(r^{\prime})\,,\ \hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}\,.

Proof.

For any
{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
, we write

g(v):=I^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)-{\mathcal{I}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)={\mathtt{g}}(c(v),v)

where

(6.8)

{\mathtt{g}}(c,v)={\mathtt{I}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(c,v)-{\mathcal{I}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{partial c Phi},\eqref{exp1Phi}}}{{=}}\partial_{c^{\hat{\jmath}}}G_{\geq 3}(c,v)=\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}}G_{\geq 3}(c,v)\\
\partial_{c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}}G_{\geq 3}(c,v)\end{pmatrix}

and
G_{\geq 3}:B_{r}(c_{*})\times B_{r}^{V}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
is defined in (
4.42
).
By (
4.43
) we have
{\mathtt{g}}(c,v)={\mathcal{O}}(\|v\|^{3})
uniformly in
c
,
proving
the first estimate (
6.6
).
Moreover, since
G_{\geq 3}(c,v)
is independent of
c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}
on
B_{r}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}}
(cf. Proposition
4.12
), it results

(6.9)

{\mathtt{g}}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}(c,v)=0\quad\text{for any}\quad v\in B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}}

and (
A.5
) implies the second
estimate (
6.6
).
Furthermore (
A.4
) and (
A.6
)
imply

(6.10)

{\rm d}_{v}{\mathtt{g}}(c,v)[\hat{v}]={\mathcal{O}}\begin{pmatrix}\|v\|(\|v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}\hat{v}\|+\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}\hat{v}\|)\\
\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|(\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}\hat{v}\|+\|v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}\hat{v}\|)\end{pmatrix}\,.

We now estimate

(6.11)

{\rm d}_{v}g(v)=({\rm d}_{v}{\mathtt{g}})(c(v),v)+(\partial_{c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}}{\mathtt{g}})(c(v),v){\rm d}_{v}c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)+(\partial_{c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}}{\mathtt{g}})(c(v),v){\rm d}_{v}c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)\,.

For any
v\in B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}}
, by Proposition
4.12
the term
{\mathtt{g}}(c,v)
in (
6.8
) satisfies

\partial_{c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}}{\mathtt{g}}(c,v)=0
and by (
6.9
) it satisfies
\partial_{c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}}{\mathtt{g}}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}(c,v)=0
.
Therefore (
A.5
) and (
4.43
) imply

(6.12)

\partial_{c^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}}{\mathtt{g}}(c,v)={\mathcal{O}}\begin{pmatrix}\|v\|^{3}\\
\|v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\end{pmatrix}\,,\qquad\partial_{c^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}}{\mathtt{g}}(c,v)={\mathcal{O}}\begin{pmatrix}\|v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\\
\|v\|\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\end{pmatrix}\,.

Then estimates (
6.7
) follow by
(
6.11
),
(
6.10
), (
6.12
) and using (
5.6
) if
v\in{\mathcal{F}}_{r^{\prime}}
, and using (
5.7
) if
v\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\hat{\jmath}}(r^{\prime})\setminus V_{\hat{\jmath}}
.
Since
I
is
C^{1}(B_{r^{\prime}}^{V})
by Lemma
6.1
the estimates
(
6.7
) hold
also on
each
V_{\hat{\jmath}}
.
∎

We now introduce the matrix valued function
A:B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2\times 2}
as

(6.13)

A(v):=\begin{pmatrix}{\rm d}_{v}I_{1}(v)[\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}_{1}(v)]&{\rm d}_{v}I_{2}(v)[\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}_{1}(v)]\\
{\rm d}_{v}I_{1}(v)[\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}_{2}(v)]&{\rm d}_{v}I_{2}(v)[\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}_{2}(v)]\end{pmatrix}

whose relevance is
highlighted by the next lemma.

Lemma 6.5
.

For any
v\in V\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}
the matrix
A(v)
is invertible if and only if

(6.14)

V=\ker({\rm d}_{v}I(v))\oplus span(\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}_{1}(v),\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}_{2}(v))\,.

Proof.

If
A(v)
is invertible,
any
\hat{v}\in V
admits the decomposition

\hat{v}=\hat{v}_{I}+\mu_{1}\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}_{1}(v)+\mu_{2}\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}_{2}(v)

where
\hat{v}_{I}\in\ker({\rm d}_{v}I(v))
and
\mu=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})^{\top}=A(v)^{-\top}{\rm d}_{v}I(v)[\hat{v}]
, thus (
6.14
) holds.
Viceversa, if (
6.14
) holds then

\rank(A(v))=\rank({\rm d}_{v}I(v))=\dim(V)-\dim(\ker({\rm d}_{v}I(v)))=2

by (
6.14
) and, noting that
\{\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}_{1}(v),\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}_{1}(v)\}
are linearly independent, by Lemma
5.3
, for any
v\in V\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}
. Therefore the
2\times 2
matrix
A(v)
is non-singular.
∎

For any
\hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}
, any
v\in B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}
, we define

(6.15)

A^{\hat{\jmath}}(v):=\begin{pmatrix}{\rm d}_{v}I^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)[\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)]&{\rm d}_{v}I^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)[\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)]\\
{\rm d}_{v}I^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)[\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)]&{\rm d}_{v}I^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)[\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)]\end{pmatrix}\,,

where
I^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v),I^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)

are introduced in
(
6.5
), i.e.
A^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)=Q_{\hat{\jmath}}A(v)Q_{\hat{\jmath}}^{-1}
. For any
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
we define

(6.16)

A(t,v):=tA(v)+(1-t){\mathcal{A}}(v)\,,\qquad A^{\hat{\jmath}}(t,v):=tA^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)+(1-t){\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)\,,

where the matrices
{\mathcal{A}}(v)
,
{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)
are defined in (
5.10
), (
5.21
).

Lemma 6.6
.

(Properties of
A(t,v)
)

The matrix
A(t,v)
defined in (
6.16
) is
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-invariant in
v
and is continuous on

{\mathbb{R}}\times B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}
, analytic in
{\mathbb{R}}\times[B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}]
and restricted to each
{\mathbb{R}}\times[(B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}})\setminus\{0\}]

for any
{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}

(where
r^{\prime}
is defined by Proposition
5.1
) and, for any
{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
,
v\in B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}
,

t\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

(6.17)

\displaystyle A^{\hat{\jmath}}(t,v)={\mathcal{O}}(1+|t|\|v\|)\|v\|^{2}\,,

\displaystyle\forall(t,v)\in{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathcal{F}}_{r^{\prime}}\,,

\displaystyle A^{\hat{\jmath}}(t,v)={\mathcal{O}}(1+|t|\|v\|)\begin{pmatrix}\|v\|^{2}&\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\\
\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}&\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\forall(t,v)\in{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathcal{N}}_{\hat{\jmath}}(r^{\prime})\,,\ \hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}\,.

There exists
r^{\prime\prime}\in(0,r^{\prime})
such that for any
(t,v)\in(-2,2)\times[B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}]
we have

(6.18)

\displaystyle\det(A(t,v))\gtrsim\|v\|^{4}

\displaystyle\text{if}\quad v\in{\mathcal{F}}_{r^{\prime\prime}}\ \text{cf.}\ \eqref{defFr}\,,

\displaystyle\det(A(t,v))=\det(A^{\hat{\jmath}}(t,v))\geq\tfrac{\delta}{4}\|v\|^{2}\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}

\displaystyle\text{if}\quad v\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\hat{\jmath}}(r^{\prime\prime})\setminus V_{\hat{\jmath}}\,,\ \hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}\,,

\displaystyle(A^{\hat{\jmath}}(t,v))_{11}\gtrsim\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v\|^{2}=\|v\|^{2}

\displaystyle\text{if}\quad v\in B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}}\,,\ \hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}\,.

Proof.

Since
{\mathcal{I}}(v)
and
I(v)

are
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-invariant (cf. Lemma
6.1
),
their gradients are
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-equivariant and so the matrix
A(t,v)
is
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-invariant.
The regularity properties of

A(t,v)
are the same of
the matrix
A(v)

in (
6.13
) which, in turn, follow by Lemma
6.1
.
In view of
(
6.15
), (
5.21
), (
6.7
),
(
4.35
), (
4.40
),
that
\Pi^{\bot}_{\hat{\jmath}}
commutes with
\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)
and
\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)
,
the matrix

R^{\hat{\jmath}}(v):=A^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)-{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)
satisfies

(6.19)

\displaystyle R^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)={\mathcal{O}}(\|v\|^{3})\,,

\displaystyle\forall v\in{\mathcal{F}}_{r^{\prime}}\,,

\displaystyle R^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)={\mathcal{O}}(\|v\|)\begin{pmatrix}\|v\|^{2}&\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\\
\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}&\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\end{pmatrix}\,,

\displaystyle\forall v\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\hat{\jmath}}(r^{\prime})\,,\ \hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}\,.

and hence
A^{\hat{\jmath}}(t,v)={\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)+t\,(A^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)-{\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v))={\mathcal{A}}^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)+tR^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)
satisfies
(
6.17
) by
(
5.24
).
Let us prove (
6.18
).
Let
R(v):=A(v)-{\mathcal{A}}(v)=Q_{\hat{\jmath}}^{-1}R^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)Q_{\hat{\jmath}}
(cf. (
6.15
) and (
5.23
)).
For any
v\in{\mathcal{F}}_{r^{\prime\prime}}

we have

d(v,{\bf V}^{{2d}})\geq\sqrt{\delta/4}\|v\|>0
and then recalling (
6.16
),

\det(A(t,v))=\underbrace{\det({\mathcal{A}}(v))}_{\gtrsim\|v\|^{4}\,\text{by}\,\eqref{lem:c away from S* estimate 30}}\det\Big(\text{Id}+\underbrace{t{\mathcal{A}}^{-1}(v)R(v)}_{={\mathcal{O}}(|t|\|v\|)\,\text{by}\,\eqref{lem:c away from S* estimate 3},\eqref{eq:inside lemma AQ = cal AQ + error}}\Big)\gtrsim\|v\|^{4}

for any
t\in(-2,2)
taking
r^{\prime\prime}\in(0,r^{\prime})
small enough, proving the first lower bound in (
6.18
). Next
we prove the second bound in (
6.18
).
By (
5.24
)
and
(
6.19
) the matrix entries of

A^{\hat{\jmath}}(t,v)
satisfy

(6.20)

\displaystyle(A^{\hat{\jmath}}(t,v))_{11}\geq\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v\|^{2}-C|t|\|v\|^{3}\,,\quad\ \,(A^{\hat{\jmath}}(t,v))_{22}\geq\big(\delta-C|t|\|v\|\big)\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\,,

\displaystyle|(A^{\hat{\jmath}}(t,v))_{12}|,\ |(A^{\hat{\jmath}}(t,v))_{21}|\leq\big(1+C|t|\|v\|\big)\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}

for some
C>0
.
We deduce

(A^{\hat{\jmath}}(t,v))_{11}(A^{\hat{\jmath}}(t,v))_{22}\geq\delta\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v\|^{2}\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}-(\delta+1)C|t|\|v\|^{3}\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}

and, assuming
C|t|\|v\|\leq 1
, that

(A^{\hat{\jmath}}(t,v))_{12}^{2}\leq\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{4}+3C|t|\|v\|^{3}\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}

so that the determinant of

A(t,v)=Q_{\hat{\jmath}}^{-1}A^{\hat{\jmath}}(t,v)Q_{\hat{\jmath}}

satisfies

(6.21)

\det(A(t,v))=\det(A^{\hat{\jmath}}(t,v))\geq(\delta\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v\|^{2}-\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2})\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}-(\delta+4)C|t|\|v\|^{3}\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\,.

Recalling
(
5.3
),
if
v\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\hat{\jmath}}(r^{\prime\prime})
then

(6.22)

\delta\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v\|^{2}-\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\geq\tfrac{\delta}{2}\|v\|^{2}\,,\quad\delta\in(0,1]\,.

Hence by (
6.21
)
and (
6.22
) we conclude that

\det(A(t,v))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{stimedis}}}{{\geq}}\Big(\tfrac{\delta}{2}-(\delta+4)C|t|\|v\|\Big)\|v\|^{2}\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\geq\tfrac{\delta}{3}\|v\|^{2}\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}

for any
v\in B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}
with
r^{\prime\prime}\in(0,r^{\prime})
small enough.
Finally the first inequality in (
6.20
) implies also
the last bound in (
6.18
)
for
r^{\prime\prime}\in(0,r^{\prime})
small enough, since
v=\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v

for any
v\in V_{\hat{\jmath}}
.
∎

The next key lemma is used to prove Theorem
6.2
and Proposition
8.4
.

Lemma 6.7
.

(Solution of
A(t,v)^{\top}\mu(v)=b(v)
)

Let
b:B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
be a
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-invariant continuous function, analytic in
B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}
and restricted to each
(B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}})\setminus\{0\}
,
{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
, satisfying

(6.23)

b^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)=Q_{\hat{\jmath}}b(v)={\mathcal{O}}\begin{pmatrix}\|v\|^{2}\\
\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\end{pmatrix}\,,\quad\forall v\in B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\,,\ {\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}\,,

where
Q_{\hat{\jmath}}
is defined in (
4.37
).
Then,
there exists a
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-invariant function
\mu:(-2,2)\times B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
solving the system

(6.24)

A(t,v)^{\top}\mu(t,v)=b(v)\,,\quad\forall(t,v)\in(-2,2)\times B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\,,

where the matrix
A(t,v)
is defined in (
6.16
) and
r^{\prime\prime}>0
is the same of Lemma
6.6
.
Moreover
the vector field

(6.25)

Z:(-2,2)\times B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\to V\,,\quad Z(t,v):=\mu(t,v)\cdot\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}(v)\,,

is
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-equivariant and satisfies the properties (

Z

) (stated in Lemma
A.3
).

Proof.

By Lemma
6.6
there is
r^{\prime\prime}\in(0,r^{\prime})
such that
the matrix
A(t,v)^{\top}
is invertible for any
(t,v)\in(-2,2)\times[B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}]
.
Then the solution of system
(
6.24
) is

(6.26)

\mu:(-2,2)\times B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\,,\ \mu(t,v):=\begin{cases}\mu_{{3d}}(t,v)&t\in(-2,2)\,,\,v\in B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}\\
\mu_{{2d}}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}\hat{\jmath}&t\in(-2,2)\,,\,v\in(B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}})\setminus\{0\}\,,\ {\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}\\
0&t\in(-2,2)\,,\,v=0\,,\end{cases}

where

(6.27)

\displaystyle\mu_{{3d}}:(-2,2)\times[B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}]\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\,,\quad(t,v)\mapsto\mu_{{3d}}(t,v):=(A(t,v))^{-\top}b(v)\,

(6.28)

\displaystyle\mu^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{{2d}}:(-2,2)\times[(B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}})\setminus\{0\}]\to{\mathbb{R}}\,,\quad(t,v)\mapsto\mu^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{{2d}}(t,v):=(A^{\hat{\jmath}}(t,v))_{11}^{-1}b^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)\,,\ {\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}\,,

where
(A^{\hat{\jmath}}(t,v))_{11}\gtrsim\|v\|^{2}
for
v\in(B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}})\setminus\{0\}
by (
6.18
).

Step 1:

The function
\mu(t,v)
satisfies the following properties:

(i)

\mu(t,v)
is analytic in
(-2,2)\times[B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}]
and restricted to
(-2,2)\times[(B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}})\setminus\{0\}]
, for any
{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
;

(ii)

\mu(t,v)
is
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-invariant
and bounded;

(iii)

\mu^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(t,v)
for any
{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
is continuous on
(-2,2)\times[B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{{\hat{\jmath}}}\setminus\{0\}]
, cf. (
5.8
).

The functions
\mu_{{3d}}

and
\mu^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{{2d}}
in (
6.27
)-(
6.28
) are analytic by
the analyticity properties of

A(t,v)
of
Lemma
6.6
and
the analyticity
assumptions on
b(v)
.
Furthermore
\mu(t,v)
is
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-invariant as
b(v)
(by assumption) and
A(t,v)
are invariant as well.
The estimates
(
6.17
)-(
6.18
) imply that

(6.29)

\displaystyle A(t,v)^{-\top}={\mathcal{O}}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{\|v\|^{2}}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\forall(t,v)\in(-2,2)\times{\mathcal{F}}_{r^{\prime\prime}}\,,

\displaystyle(A^{\hat{\jmath}}(t,v))^{-\top}={\mathcal{O}}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{\|v\|^{2}}&\frac{1}{\|v\|^{2}}\\
\frac{1}{\|v\|^{2}}&\frac{1}{\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\forall(t,v)\in(-2,2)\times[{\mathcal{N}}_{\hat{\jmath}}(r^{\prime\prime})\setminus V_{\hat{\jmath}}]\,,\ \hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}\,,

\displaystyle(A^{\hat{\jmath}}(t,v))_{11}^{-1}={\mathcal{O}}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v\|^{2}}\end{pmatrix}={\mathcal{O}}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{\|v\|^{2}}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\forall(t,v)\in(-2,2)\times[(B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}})\setminus\{0\}]\,,

and jointly with
the estimates (
6.23
) on
b(v)
, we deduce
that
\mu_{{3d}}(v)
and

\mu^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}_{2d}(v)

in (
6.27
)-(
6.28
)
are uniformly bounded.
Let us prove
(iii)
.
Let
(t_{i},v_{i})\to(\bar{t},\bar{v})\in(-2,2)\times[(B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}})\setminus\{0\}]
.
For any subsequence
(t_{i_{m}},v_{i_{m}})
there exists another subsequence
(t_{i_{m_{\ell}}},v_{i_{m_{\ell}}})
and
\bar{\mu}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
such that
\mu(t_{i_{m_{\ell}}},v_{i_{m_{\ell}}})\to\bar{\mu}
.
Passing to the limit in (
6.24
) we deduce that
\bar{\mu}
solves

A(\bar{t},\bar{v})^{\top}\bar{\mu}=b(\bar{v})
, thus

A^{\hat{\jmath}}(\bar{t},\bar{v})^{\top}\bar{\mu}^{\hat{\jmath}}=b^{\hat{\jmath}}(\bar{v})
for any

\hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}
, thus, by (
6.17
),

(A(\bar{t},\bar{v}))_{11}\bar{\mu}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}=b^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(\bar{v})\quad\text{where}\quad\bar{\mu}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}=\bar{\mu}\cdot\hat{\jmath}\quad\text{(cf. \eqref{eq:compoc})}

and hence
\bar{\mu}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}=\mu_{{2d}}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(\bar{t},\bar{v})
in (
6.28
).
The proof of
(iii)
is concluded.

Step 2:

The vector field
Z(t,v)

in (
6.25
)
satisfies properties (

Z

).

Since
\mu(t,v)
is
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-invariant
and
\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}(v)
is

{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-equivariant, the vector field
Z(t,v)
in (
6.25
) is
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-equivariant as well.
In view of (
4.35
) we have

(6.30)

Z(t,v)=-\sum_{{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}}\mu(t,v)\cdot\hat{\jmath}\,\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v=-\sum_{{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}}\mu^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(t,v)\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v\,.

Item

(iii)
implies that

Z(t,v)
is continuous on
(-2,2)\times(B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\setminus\{0\})
and

(ii)
implies that

\|Z(t,v)\|\lesssim\|v\|
thus it is continuous
also at
v=0
.
Properties (

Z

)-
(1)
and
(3)
are proved.
Property (

Z

)-
(2)
follows from
(i)
, and (

Z

)-
(4)
from (
6.30
).
∎

6.2
Rectification of the momentum

To prove Theorem
6.2

we implement a suitable Moser’s trick.
For any
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
v\in B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}
let

(6.31)

I(t,v):=tI(v)+(1-t){\mathcal{I}}(v)\,.

We look for a family of equivariant homeomorphisms
\zeta(t,\cdot)
satisfying
(
6.3
), (
6.4
) and

(6.32)

I(t,\cdot)\circ\zeta(t,\cdot):=I(0,\cdot)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{Def ItQ}}}{{=}}{\mathcal{I}}(\cdot)\,,\quad\forall t\in[0,1]\,,\quad\zeta(0,\cdot)=\text{Id}\,,

so that at
t=1
the map

\zeta(1,\cdot)

solves (
6.2
).
We look for
\zeta(t,\cdot)
as the flow

\partial_{t}\zeta(t,v)=X(t,\zeta(t,v))\,,\quad\zeta(0,v)=v\,,

generated by a time dependent vector field of the form

(6.33)

X:(-2,2)\times B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\to V\,,\quad X(t,v):=\mu(t,v)\cdot\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}(v)\,,

for some
\mu:(-2,2)\times B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2}

and
r^{\prime\prime}\in(0,r^{\prime})
sufficiently small.
By differentiating in
t
, (
6.32
) is equivalent to

(6.34)

{\rm d}_{v}I(t,v)[X(t,v)]+(I-{\mathcal{I}})(v)=0\,,

that, by (
6.33
) and recalling (
6.16
),
(
6.13
), (
6.15
), (
6.31
), amounts to the system

(6.35)

A^{\top}(t,v)\mu(t,v)={\mathcal{I}}(v)-I(v)

where
A(t,v)
is the matrix defined
in (
6.16
).
Lemmata
6.1
,
6.4
imply that

b(v):={\mathcal{I}}(v)-I(v)
satisfies the assumptions of Lemma
6.7

and
we deduce the existence of
r^{\prime\prime}\in(0,r^{\prime})
and

\mu:(-2,2)\times B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
,
such that the vector-field (
6.33
) solves (
6.35
) and satisfies the assumptions of Lemma
A.3
.
Therefore Lemma
A.3
implies that the vector-field
X(t,v)
in (
6.33
) generates
flow
\zeta(t,v)
on
B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}
locally in time, well defined in
[0,1]\times B_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}^{V}

for some
r^{\prime\prime\prime}\in(0,r^{\prime\prime})
.
We set
U_{1}:=B_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}^{V}
.
By the Brouwer theorem on invariance of domains we deduce that

(6.36)

U_{2}:=\zeta(1,U_{1})\subset B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\quad\text{is open}\,.

In view of Remark
4.9
and the regularity properties of
X(t,v)
(specifically (

Z

)-
(2)
) we deduce (
6.3
)-(
6.4
). The proof
is concluded.

7
Topology of
\mathbb{M}_{a}

In this section we carefully
describe the topology of
the level sets

(7.1)

\mathbb{M}_{a}:=\Big\{v\in U_{2}\ |\ I(v)=a\Big\}\,,\quad a\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\,,

where
I(v)
is the reduced momentum in (
6.1
)
and

U_{2}
is the open neighborhood of
0
defined in Theorem
6.2
.
To start
note that

\mathbb{M}_{a}

is a
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-space
equipped with the linear action
(\tau_{\theta})

in (
1.11
).
Furthermore

Lemma 7.1
.

\mathbb{M}_{a}^{3d}:=\mathbb{M}_{a}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}
is a manifold and

(7.2)

V=\underbrace{T_{v}\mathbb{M}_{a}^{{3d}}}_{=\ker{\rm d}_{v}I(v)}\oplus\,\text{span}(\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}_{1}(v),\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}_{2}(v))\,,\quad\forall v\in\mathbb{M}_{a}^{3d}\,.

Proof.

The set
\mathbb{M}_{a}^{{3d}}\subset U_{2}\subset B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}
by Remark
6.3
and, for any

v\in\mathbb{M}_{a}^{3d}
, Lemma
6.6
implies that the matrix
A(v)=A(1,v)
in (
6.13
), (
6.16
) is invertible.
Therefore
\{{\rm d}_{v}I_{1}(v),{\rm d}_{v}I_{2}(v)\}
are linearly independent and by Lemma
6.5
we deduce (
7.2
).
∎

For any
a\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
we consider the orthogonal
decomposition

(7.3)

V=V_{-}\oplus V_{0}\oplus V_{+}

where

(7.4)

V_{-}:=\bigoplus_{\hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{-}}V_{\hat{\jmath}}\,,\quad V_{0}:=\bigoplus_{\hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{0}}V_{\hat{\jmath}}\,,\quad V_{+}:=\bigoplus_{\hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{+}}V_{\hat{\jmath}}\,,

and

{\mathcal{D}}_{-}:=\big\{\hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}\,|\,\hat{\jmath}\cdot a^{\bot}<0\big\}\,,\quad{\mathcal{D}}_{0}:=\big\{\hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}\,|\,\hat{\jmath}\cdot a^{\bot}=0\big\}\,,\quad{\mathcal{D}}_{+}:=\big\{\hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}\,|\,\hat{\jmath}\cdot a^{\bot}>0\big\}\,.

The subspaces

V_{-},V_{0},V_{+}
are invariant
under the linear action
\tau_{\theta}
in (
1.11
).

Remark 7.2
.

If
a\in\interior({\mathcal{C}})
is in the interior of
the cone defined in (
1.26
)
then

{\mathcal{D}}_{\pm}\not=\varnothing
and so

\dim(V_{\pm})\geq 2
.
If

a\in\partial{\mathcal{C}}\setminus\{0\}

then

{\mathcal{D}}_{0}\not=\varnothing
, so

\dim(V_{0})\geq 2
, and
either
{\mathcal{D}}_{-}=\varnothing
or
{\mathcal{D}}_{+}=\varnothing
.

We denote
S(V_{-})
,
S(V_{0})
,
S(V_{+})
the unit spheres
in
V_{-},V_{0},V_{+}
.

Theorem 7.3
.

(Topology of
\mathbb{M}_{a}
)

If
a\not\in{\mathcal{C}}

then
\mathbb{M}_{a}=\varnothing
.
If
a=0
then
\mathbb{M}_{a}=\{0\}
.
Moreover there exists
\varepsilon>0
such that for any
a\in{\mathcal{C}}\cap\overline{B_{\varepsilon}}\setminus\{0\}
the following holds:

(\beth)

(Boundary case)
If
a\in\partial{\mathcal{C}}\setminus\{0\}
then

\mathbb{M}_{a}\subset V_{0}
is a submanifold of
V_{0}
which is
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-equivariantly diffeomorphic to the unit sphere

S(V_{0})
, i.e.

\mathbb{M}_{a}\cong S(V_{0})
.

(\daleth)

(Interior case)
If
a\in\interior({\mathcal{C}})
then two cases may occur:

(\daleth
1)

(Non-collinear case)
If
a\not\parallel\hat{\jmath}
for any
\hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}
, then
\mathbb{M}_{a}
is a closed submanifold of
V
contained in
V\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}
which is

{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-equivariantly diffeomorphic to
S(V_{-})\times S(V_{+})
.

(\daleth
2)

(Collinear case)
If
a\parallel\hat{\jmath}_{0}
is parallel to some resonant wave direction
\hat{\jmath}_{0}\in{\mathcal{D}}
then

(7.5)

\mathbb{M}_{a}=\mathbb{M}_{a}^{{3d}}\,\sqcup\,\mathbb{M}_{a}^{{2d}}

where

\mathbb{M}_{a}^{{3d}}:=\mathbb{M}_{a}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}=\mathbb{M}_{a}\setminus V_{0}
is a submanifold of
V
and

\mathbb{M}_{a}^{{2d}}:=\mathbb{M}_{a}\cap V_{0}
is a submanifold of
V_{0}
diffeomorphic to the unit sphere
S(V_{0})

(note that
V_{0}=V_{\hat{\jmath}_{0}}
and
\dim(V_{0})\geq 2
). There is a
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-equivariant homeomorphism

(7.6)

\gamma:\mathbb{M}_{a}\toup^{\cong}[S(V_{-})\times S(V_{+})]\star S(V_{0})\,,\quad\gamma(\mathbb{M}_{a}^{{2d}})=S(V_{0})\,,

where
\star
denotes the
join product
(
2.39
) and
\dim(V_{\pm})\geq 2
.

The case
(\daleth
1) was proved in
[
15
]
. The novel cases are
(\beth)
and
(\daleth
2).

We now prove
Theorem
7.3
.
The straightening Theorem
6.2
implies that
I(v)={\mathcal{I}}(\zeta^{-1}(v))
for any
v\in U_{2}
.
By (
4.34
) the image of
{\mathcal{I}}:V\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
is the cone
{\mathcal{C}}
in (
1.26
)
and therefore, if
a\not\in{\mathcal{C}}
we deduce that
\mathbb{M}_{a}=\varnothing
.
Furthermore
if
a=0
then
{\mathcal{I}}^{-1}(a)=0
and so

\mathbb{M}_{a}=\{0\}
.

Lemma 7.4
.

There exists
\varepsilon>0
such that, for any
|a|\leq\varepsilon
,

(7.7)

{\mathcal{I}}^{-1}(a)\subset U_{1}\quad\text{and}\quad\overline{D_{\varepsilon}}\subset U_{2}\quad\text{where}\quad D_{\varepsilon}:=\{v\in U_{2}\,|\,|I(v)|\leq\varepsilon\}\,.

Proof.

If
v\in{\mathcal{I}}^{-1}(a)
then,
by (
4.34
) and (
1.24
)
we deduce

\sum_{{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}}\frac{\omega(j)}{2|j|}\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v\|^{2}\leq|a||c_{*}|
. Since
\omega(j)>0

we deduce (
7.7
)
for any
|a|\leq\varepsilon
small enough.
∎

For any
a\in{\mathcal{C}}\cap\overline{B_{\varepsilon}}

the set
\mathbb{M}_{a}
in (
7.1
) is the non-empty compact set
\mathbb{M}_{a}=\zeta({\mathcal{I}}^{-1}(a))
, and
it is then sufficient to characterize the level sets of the quadratic momentum

{\mathcal{I}}(v)
in (
4.34
).

Lemma 7.5
.

(Level sets of
{\mathcal{I}}
)

If
a\in{\mathcal{C}}\setminus\{0\}
then

(\beth)

If
a\in\partial{\mathcal{C}}\setminus\{0\}
then
{\mathcal{I}}^{-1}(a)\subset V_{0}
is
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-equivariantly diffeomorphic to
S(V_{0})
.

(\daleth)

If
a\in\interior({\mathcal{C}})\setminus\{0\}
then the following exclusive cases may occur:

(\daleth
1)

If
a\not\parallel\hat{\jmath}
for any
\hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}
then
{\mathcal{I}}^{-1}(a)
is a closed submanifold of
V
contained in
V\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}
which is

{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-equivariantly diffeomorphic to
S(V_{-})\times S(V_{+})
.

(\daleth
2)

If
a\parallel\hat{\jmath}_{0}
for some resonant wave direction
\hat{\jmath}_{0}\in{\mathcal{D}}

then

{\mathcal{I}}^{-1}(a)\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}={\mathcal{I}}^{-1}(a)\setminus V_{0}

is a submanifold of
V
,

{\mathcal{I}}^{-1}(a)\cap{\bf V}^{{2d}}={\mathcal{I}}^{-1}(a)\cap V_{0}
is a submanifold of
V_{0}=V_{\hat{\jmath}_{0}}
, diffeomorphic to

S(V_{0})

and there is a
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-equivariant homeomorphism

(7.8)

\gamma_{1}:{\mathcal{I}}^{-1}(a)\toup^{\cong}[S(V_{-})\times S(V_{+})]\star S(V_{0})\,,\quad\gamma_{1}({\mathcal{I}}^{-1}(a){\cap{\bf V}^{2d}})=S(V_{0})\,.

Proof.

Projecting
{\mathcal{I}}(v)
in (
4.34
)
along
a
and

a^{\bot}
,
we write

(7.9)

{\mathcal{I}}^{-1}(a)=\Big\{v\in V\ |\ \sum_{\hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}}\frac{-\hat{\jmath}\cdot a}{2|a|^{2}}\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v\|^{2}=1\,,\ \sum_{\hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}}\frac{-\hat{\jmath}\cdot a^{\bot}}{2|a|^{2}}\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v\|^{2}=0\Big\}\,.

By (
1.27
),
we have
a\cdot c_{*}<0
and
summing the second equation in (
7.9
) multiplied by
\frac{a^{\bot}\cdot c_{*}}{a\cdot c_{*}}
to the first equation,
and decomposing
c_{*}=a(c_{*}\cdot a)|a|^{-2}+a^{\bot}(c_{*}\cdot a^{\bot})|a|^{-2}
, we deduce that

(7.10)

{\mathcal{I}}^{-1}(a)=\Big\{v\in V\ |\ {\mathcal{B}}(v)=1\,,\ {\mathcal{K}}(v)=0\Big\}

where

(7.11)

{\mathcal{B}}(v):=\frac{1}{2|a\cdot c_{*}|}\sum_{\hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}}c_{*}\cdot\hat{\jmath}\,\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v\|^{2}\,,\quad{\mathcal{K}}(v):=\sum_{\hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}}\hat{\jmath}\cdot a^{\bot}\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v\|^{2}\,.

Note that
c_{*}\cdot\hat{\jmath}>0
for any
\hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}
(cf. (
1.24
)) and hence
\sqrt{{\mathcal{B}}(v)}
is a norm on
V
.

We define the
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-equivariant diffeomorphism

(7.12)

\xi:V\setminus\{0\}\to V\setminus\{0\}\,,\quad\xi(v):=g(v)\psi(v)\,,\quad g(v):=\frac{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|v|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}{\sqrt{{\mathcal{B}}(\psi(v))}}\,,

where
\psi:V\to V

is the linear isomorphism

(7.13)

\psi(v):=\sum_{\hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{-}}|\hat{\jmath}\cdot a^{\bot}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v+\sum_{\hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{0}}\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v+\sum_{\hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{+}}(\hat{\jmath}\cdot{a^{\bot}})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}v

(it is well defined in view of (
7.4
))
and

(7.14)

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|v|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}:=\tfrac{1}{2}\|\Pi_{-}v\|^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}\|\Pi_{+}v\|^{2}+\|\Pi_{0}v\|^{2}\,,

denoting

\Pi_{\pm}:V\to V_{\pm}
and
\Pi_{0}:V\to V_{0}

the orthogonal projectors associated to the decomposition (
7.3
).
Note that
g(v)
in (
7.12
) is a
positive,
C^{\infty}(V\setminus\{0\},{\mathbb{R}})

homogenous function
of degree
0
.
Under the diffeomorphism
\xi
in (
7.12
)
the functions
{\mathcal{B}}
and
{\mathcal{K}}
in (
7.11
) transform into

(7.15)

({\mathcal{B}}\circ\xi)(v)=|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|v|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}\,,\quad({\mathcal{K}}\circ\xi)(v)=g^{2}(v)\big(\|\Pi_{+}v\|^{2}-\|\Pi_{-}v\|^{2}\big)\,,\quad\forall v\in V\setminus\{0\}\,.

As a consequence of (
7.15
) and (
7.14
), the map

\xi
defines a

{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-equivariant diffeomorphism which maps the set
{\mathcal{I}}^{-1}(a)

in (
7.10
) onto

(7.16)

\Sigma:=\Big\{v\in V\ |\ \tfrac{1}{2}\|\Pi_{-}v\|^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}\|\Pi_{+}v\|^{2}+\|\Pi_{0}v\|^{2}=1\,,\ \|\Pi_{+}v\|^{2}=\|\Pi_{-}v\|^{2}\Big\}\,.

(\beth)

Case
a\in\partial{\mathcal{C}}\setminus\{0\}
.
In view of Remark
7.2
,
either
{\mathcal{D}}_{-}
or
{\mathcal{D}}_{+}
is empty, say
{\mathcal{D}}_{-}=\varnothing
. Then
the set
\Sigma
in (
7.16
)
reduces to

\Sigma=\{v\in V\ |\ \Pi_{+}v=0\,,\ \|\Pi_{0}v\|^{2}=1\}\equiv S(V_{0})
and

\xi:S(V_{0})\to{\mathcal{I}}^{-1}(a)=\xi(S(V_{0}))\subset V_{0}

(the map
\psi
in (
7.13
), thus
\xi
, maps
V_{0}\setminus\{0\}\to V_{0}\setminus\{0\}
) is a
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-equivariant diffeomorphism.

(\daleth)

Case
a\in\interior({\mathcal{C}})
.
Remark
7.2
shows that

\dim(V_{\pm})\geq 2
.
We distinguish two sub-cases:

(\daleth
1)

Assume
a\not\parallel\hat{\jmath}
for any
\hat{\jmath}\in{\mathcal{D}}
. Then
{\mathcal{D}}_{0}=\varnothing
and
(
7.16
) reduces to

\Sigma=\{v\in V\ |\ \|\Pi_{-}v\|^{2}=\|\Pi_{+}v\|^{2}=1\}

\equiv S(V_{-})\times S(V_{+})\subset V\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}
. Hence
\xi:S(V_{-})\times S(V_{+})\to{\mathcal{I}}^{-1}(a)

is a
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-equivariant diffeomorphism and Remark
4.9
implies
{\mathcal{I}}^{-1}(a)\subset V\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}
.

(\daleth
2)

Assume
a\parallel\hat{\jmath}_{0}
for some
\hat{\jmath}_{0}\in{\mathcal{D}}
.
Since the diffeomorphism

\xi
in (
7.12
) maps each

V_{\hat{\jmath}}\setminus\{0\}
in itself and

V\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}
in itself, we have

\xi:\underbrace{\Sigma\cap{\bf V}^{{2d}}}_{=S(V_{0})\,\text{by}\,\eqref{eq:Sigma}}\to\underbrace{{\mathcal{I}}^{-1}(a)\cap{\bf V}^{{2d}}}_{={\mathcal{I}}^{-1}(a)\cap V_{0}}\,,\quad\xi:\Sigma\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}\to{\mathcal{I}}^{-1}(a)\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}={\mathcal{I}}^{-1}(a)\setminus V_{0}\,,

and
{\mathcal{I}}^{-1}(a)\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}
is a sub-manifold of
V
of codimension two because,
for any
v\in V\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}
the vectors
\{\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}_{1}(v),\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}_{2}(v)\}
are linearly independent
by Lemma
5.3
.
We now prove that

\Sigma

is homeomorphic to the join
topological space
M\star Y

where
M:=S(V_{-})\times S(V_{+})
and

Y:=S(V_{0})
.
The map

f:M\times Y\times[0,1]\to\Sigma\,,\ (v_{1},v_{0},t)\mapsto f(v_{1},v_{0},t):=\sqrt{1-t^{2}}\,v_{1}+{t}\,v_{0}\,,

is
continuous, surjective,
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-equivariant,
and defines a map
\tilde{f}
on the quotient space

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M\times Y\times[0,1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M\star Y=\frac{M\times Y\times[0,1]}{\sim}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

where
\sim
is the equivalence relation defined in (
2.40
).
The map
\tilde{f}
is continuous, bijective and since
M,Y
are compact it is a
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-equivariant homeomorphism between

M\star Y
and
\Sigma
. The homeomorphism (
7.8
) is

\gamma_{1}:=\tilde{f}^{-1}\circ\xi^{-1}
(note that
\tilde{f}(Y)=S(V_{0})
).
∎

8
Existence and multiplicity of
3d
Stokes waves

In this section we finally prove Theorem
1.1
regarding
existence and multiplicity of truly
3d
Stokes waves
having
momentum

{\mathcal{I}}(u)=a
,

collinear
with exactly
one
resonant wave vector
j\in{\mathcal{V}}
. Specifically
we take any

(8.1)

a\in\interior({\mathcal{C}})\cap\overline{B_{\varepsilon}}\quad\text{where}\quad\varepsilon>0\ \text{is fixed as in Theorem }\ref{Topology of Sa}

(i.e. as in Lemma

7.4
), and

(8.2)

a\parallel j_{0}\ \text{for some}\ j_{0}\in{\mathcal{V}}\,,\quad a\not\parallel j\,,\ \forall j\in{\mathcal{V}}\setminus\{j_{0}\}\,.

In this case
the
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-space

\mathbb{M}_{a}
in
(
7.1
) is
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-equivariantly homeomorphic to

[S(V_{-})\times S(V_{+})]\star S^{1}

where
S^{1}\equiv S(V_{0})
because

\dim(V_{0})=2
,
and Theorem

7.3
-(
\daleth 2
) implies that

•

(topology of
\mathbb{M}_{a}
)

the compact
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-space
\mathbb{M}_{a}
is the disjoint union

(8.3)

\mathbb{M}_{a}=\underbrace{\mathbb{M}_{a}^{3d}}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\gamma}}{{\cong}}S(V_{-})\times S(V_{+})}\sqcup\underbrace{\mathbb{M}_{a}^{2d}}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\gamma}}{{\cong}}S(V_{0})\equiv S^{1}}\,,\qquad\mathbb{M}_{a}^{3d}=\mathbb{M}_{a}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}\,,\ \mathbb{M}_{a}^{2d}=\mathbb{M}_{a}\cap V_{0}\,,

where
V_{0}=V_{j_{0}}
and

\dim(V_{\pm})\geq 2
.
The set of
3d
-waves

\mathbb{M}_{a}^{3d}
is a manifold
and (
7.2
) holds.
The linear action
(\tau_{\theta})_{\theta\in{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}}
in (
1.11
)
acts transitively
on
\mathbb{M}_{a}^{2d}
(it
consists of only
one

{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-orbit) and, for any

v
in
\mathbb{M}_{a}^{{3d}}

the stabilizer
({\mathbb{T}}_{\Gamma}^{2})_{v}
, cf. (
4.26
), are finite.

Remark 8.1
.

For any
v\in\mathbb{M}_{a}^{2d}
then
u=v+w(c_{{2d}}^{\parallel_{\hat{\jmath}}}(v)\hat{\jmath}+c_{*}^{\bot_{\hat{\jmath}}}\hat{\jmath}^{\bot},v)
is the celebrated
2d
Stokes wave constructed by the Crandall-Rabinowitz bifurcation theorem having momentum
{\mathcal{I}}(u)=a
.

We first prove the existence of at least one
3d
Stokes wave by a simple variational argument.
Then, to prove multiplicity, we
construct in Proposition
8.4
a gradient-like flow of the metric space
\mathbb{M}_{a}
, whose stationary points
are Stokes waves and
we exploit the rich equivariant topology of
\mathbb{M}_{a}
described in (
8.3
) via the abstract Theorem
3.14
.
Clearly
the existence result is covered by Theorem
1.1
, but we find instructive to include it.
Finally we
prove Theorem
1.3
.

Existence of at least one
3d
-Stokes wave.

We define the reduced Hamiltonian

(8.4)

H:B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\to{\mathbb{R}}\,,\quad v\mapsto H(v):={\mathtt{H}}(c(v),v)\,,

where
c(v)
is defined in Proposition
5.1
and

(8.5)

\mathtt{H}(c,v):={\mathcal{H}}(v+w(c,v))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{def reduced functional},\eqref{eq:def I(c,v)}}}{{=}}\Phi(c,v)-c\cdot{\mathtt{I}}(c,v)\,,\quad\forall(c,v)\in B_{r}(c_{*})\times B_{r}^{V}\,,

constrained to
\mathbb{M}_{a}
.

Lemma 8.2
.

The reduced Hamiltonian
H(v)
in (
8.4
) is
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
-invariant and of class
C^{1}(B_{r^{\prime}}^{V})
.

Proof.

The function
c(v)
in
Proposition
5.1
is
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
-invariant and
{\mathtt{H}}(c,v)
is
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
-invariant and
satisfies the assumptions of Lemma
5.14
, by Proposition
4.12
.
∎

Lemma 8.3
.

(Natural constraint)
If
\bar{v}\in{\mathbb{M}_{a}^{3d}}
is a constrained critical point of
H_{|\mathbb{M}_{a}}:\mathbb{M}_{a}\to{\mathbb{R}}

then
\bar{u}:=\bar{v}+w(c(\bar{v}),\bar{v})
is a 3d-Stokes wave solution of
{\mathcal{F}}(c(\bar{v}),\bar{u})=0

with momentum
{\mathcal{I}}(\bar{u})=a
.

Proof.

We differentiate the function
H(v)
in (
8.4
)
that,
in view of (
8.5
) and (
6.1
), is equal to

H(v)=\Phi(c(v),v)-c(v)\cdot I(v)
.
For any
v\in B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}
and any
\hat{v}\in V
we get

(8.6)

{\rm d}_{v}H(v)[\hat{v}]=\underbrace{\big(\partial_{c}\Phi(c(v),v)-I(v)\big)}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{partial c Phi},\eqref{Def I dritto}}}{{=}}0}\cdot{\rm d}_{v}c(v)[\hat{v}]+({\rm d}_{v}\Phi)(c(v),v)[\hat{v}]-c(v)\cdot{\rm d}_{v}I(v)[\hat{v}]\,.

Since
\bar{v}\in\mathbb{M}_{a}^{{3d}}=\mathbb{M}_{a}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}
is a critical point of
H_{|\mathbb{M}_{a}}

and
T_{\bar{v}}\mathbb{M}_{a}^{{3d}}=\ker{\rm d}_{v}I(\bar{v})
, cf. (
7.2
), we have

(8.7)

0={\rm d}_{v}H(\bar{v})_{|T_{\bar{v}}\mathbb{M}_{a}^{{3d}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:the usual computations}}}{{=}}({\rm d}_{v}\Phi)(c(\bar{v}),\bar{v})_{|T_{\bar{v}}\mathbb{M}_{a}^{{3d}}}\,.

By the splitting (
7.2
), the fact that

c(v)=c_{3d}(v)
solves (
5.1
) by Proposition
5.1
, and
(
8.7
)
we conclude that
({\rm d}_{v}\Phi)(c(\bar{v}),\bar{v})=0
. Then
Lemma
4.4
implies that

{\mathcal{F}}(c(\bar{v}),\bar{v}+w(c(\bar{v}),\bar{v}))=0
.
∎

By Lemma
8.3
, it is enough to find a critical point
\bar{v}\in\mathbb{M}_{a}^{{3d}}
of
H_{|\mathbb{M}_{a}}
.
Since
\mathbb{M}_{a}^{{2d}}

is a

{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-orbit (see comments below (
8.3
)) and
H
is
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-invariant, it is contained in a level of
H_{|\mathbb{M}_{a}}
, namely

H(\mathbb{M}_{a}^{{2d}})=\ell_{*}
.
Since
\mathbb{M}_{a}
is compact,
H_{|\mathbb{M}_{a}}
attains
minimum, say
m
, and maximum, say
M
, on
\mathbb{M}_{a}
.
If
m=M
the function
H_{|\mathbb{M}_{a}}
is constant and hence
any
point of
\mathbb{M}_{a}^{{3d}}
is critical.
If
m<M
then, either
\ell_{*}\not=m
or

\ell_{*}\not=M
, and so
there exists at least
one
critical
point
\bar{v}
of
H_{|\mathbb{M}_{a}}
in
\mathbb{M}_{a}^{{3d}}
.

Gradient-like flow.

In order to prove multiplicity results,
we first construct a
gradient-like flow for the
reduced Hamiltonian
H
defined in (
8.4
).

Proposition 8.4
.

(Gradient-like flow)

There exists a global flow
\phi:{\mathbb{R}}\times D_{\varepsilon}\to D_{\varepsilon}
where
D_{\varepsilon}
is defined in (
7.7
)
such that, for any
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

(i)

I(\phi^{t}(v))=I(v)
for
any
v\in D_{\varepsilon}
, any
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
;

(ii)

\phi^{t}
is
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-equivariant and

(8.8)

\phi^{t}(D_{\varepsilon}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}})=D_{\varepsilon}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}\,,\quad\phi^{t}(D_{\varepsilon}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}})=D_{\varepsilon}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}}\,,\quad\forall{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}\,;

(iii)

for any
v\in D_{\varepsilon}

(8.9)

H(\phi^{t}(v))=H(v)-\int_{0}^{t}\Big\|(\nabla_{v}\Phi)\big(c(\bar{v}),\bar{v}\big)\big|_{\bar{v}=\phi^{\tau}(v)}\Big\|^{2}{\rm d}\tau\,.

Moreover
H
is strictly decreasing on non-constant trajectories of
\phi
.

In view of
(i)
the set
\mathbb{M}_{a}
in (
7.1
) is invariant under
\phi^{t}
and
(iii)
implies the following corollary.

Lemma 8.5
.

If
\bar{v}\in\mathbb{M}_{a}^{{3d}}

is a stationary point of the gradient-like flow
\phi^{t}_{|\mathbb{M}_{a}}
,
then
\bar{u}:=\bar{v}+w(c(\bar{v}),\bar{v})
is a
3d
Stokes wave solution of
{\mathcal{F}}(c(\bar{v}),\bar{u})=0
with momentum
{\mathcal{I}}(\bar{u})=a
.

To prove Proposition
8.4
we look for
\phi^{t}(\cdot)
as the flow generated by a vector field of the form

(8.10)

Y(v):=-(\nabla_{v}\Phi)(c(v),v)+\mu(v)\cdot\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}(v)\,,\quad\forall v\in B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\,,

where

\mu(v)
is determined by the condition

(8.11)

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}I(\phi^{t}(v))=0\quad\text{i.e.}\quad{\rm d}_{v}I(v)[Y(v)]=0\,.

By (
8.10
)
system (
8.11
) is equivalent to

(8.12)

A^{\top}(v)\mu(v)=b(v)\qquad\text{where}\qquad b(v):={\rm d}_{v}I(v)[(\nabla_{v}\Phi)(c(v),v)]\

and
A(v)
is the matrix in
(
6.13
).

Lemma 8.6
.

There is
a solution
\mu:B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
of system (
8.12
) where
r^{\prime\prime}>0
is fixed in Lemma
6.6
.
The corresponding vector field
Y(v)
in (
8.10
) satisfies the properties (

Z

) of Lemma
A.3
.

Proof.

We solve (
8.12
) applying Lemma
6.7
with
t=1
.
Let us verify
its assumptions. The
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
invariance of
b(v)
follows
form the invariance of
I(v)
and
\Phi(c,v)
. Moreover
Proposition
4.12
and Lemma
5.12
imply that the map
v\mapsto(\nabla_{v}\Phi)(c(v),v)
is continuous on
B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}
, analytic in
B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}
and restricted to each
(B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\cap V_{\hat{\jmath}})\setminus\{0\}
, for any
{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}

(thus satisfies properties (

Z

)-
(1)
-
(2)
).
Therefore, using also Lemma
6.1
, the map

b:B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
satisfies the same properties as well. Let us verify also (
6.23
). Since the function
\Phi(c,v)
satisfies the assumptions of Lemma

A.2
with
m=2
, we deduce by
(
A.4
) that,
for any
v\in B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}
,

(8.13)

\|(\nabla_{v}\Phi)(c(v),v)\|\lesssim\|v\|\,,\qquad\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}\nabla_{v}\Phi(c(v),v)\|\lesssim\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|\,,\quad\forall{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}\,,

in particular
(\nabla_{v}\Phi)(c(v),v)
satisfies (

Z

)-
(3)
-
(4)
.
Hence, for any
{\hat{\jmath}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
,
v\in B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}
, we deduce

b^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)=Q_{\hat{\jmath}}b(v)={\rm d}_{v}I^{\hat{\jmath}}(v)[(\nabla_{v}\Phi)(c(v),v)]={\mathcal{O}}\begin{pmatrix}\|v\|^{2}\\
\|\Pi_{\hat{\jmath}}^{\bot}v\|^{2}\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{by}\ \eqref{eq:Nabla cI},\eqref{Estimate on d I1Q dritto}\ \text{and}\ \eqref{eq:estimate on nabla Phi}\,.

Lemma
6.7
implies the existence of
\mu(v):=\mu(1,v)
solving (
8.12
) and such that
\mu(v)\cdot\nabla{\mathcal{I}}(v)
satisfies the assumptions (

Z

) of Lemma
A.3
.
As we already verified that
(\nabla_{v}\Phi)(c(v),v)
satisfies (

Z

), the vector field
Y(v)
satisfies properties (

Z

) as well.
∎

The flow of
Y(v)
is a Lyapunov-function for
H(v)
in (
8.4
).

Lemma 8.7
.

The vector field
Y(v)
in (
8.10
) with
\mu(v)
constructed in Lemma
8.6

satisfies

(8.14)

{\rm d}_{v}H(v)[Y(v)]=-\|(\nabla_{v}\Phi)(c(v),v)\|^{2}\,,\quad\forall v\in B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\,.

Proof.

For any
v\in B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}{\setminus{\bf V}^{2d}}\subset B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}{\setminus{\bf V}^{2d}}
, inserting
\hat{v}=Y(v)
in
(
8.6
) we get

\displaystyle{\rm d}_{v}H(v)[Y(v)]

\displaystyle=({\rm d}_{v}\Phi)(c(v),v)[Y(v)]{-}c(v)\cdot{\rm d}_{v}I(v)[Y(v)]

(8.15)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{I is conserved},\eqref{Def Y field}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle({\rm d}_{v}\Phi)(c(v),v)[{-}(\nabla_{v}\Phi)(c(v),v)+\mu(v)\cdot\nabla_{v}{\mathcal{I}}(v)]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{EQ c Phi}}}{{=}}-\|\nabla_{v}\Phi(c(v),v)\|^{2}

proving (
8.14
) for any
v\in B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}\setminus{\bf V}^{{2d}}
.
Since
{\rm d}_{v}H(v)
,
Y(v)
and
(\nabla_{v}\Phi)(c(v),v)
are continuous in
v
, we deduce by (
8.15
) that
(
8.14
)
holds for any

v\in B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
8.4
.

By Lemma
A.3
, the vector field
Y(v)

in Lemma
8.6
admits
a

{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-equivariant local in time
flow
\phi^{t}(v)
for any
v\in B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}
, that is globally defined on
D_{\varepsilon}
. Indeed,
by (
8.11
)
the restricted
momentum
I(\cdot)

is conserved by the flow
\phi
.
Furthermore

\overline{D_{\varepsilon}}\subset U_{2}\subset B_{r^{\prime\prime}}^{V}

by (
7.7
) and Remark
6.3
(
\varepsilon
is fixed in Lemma
7.4
).
Since the set
D_{\varepsilon}
is
compact
and
invariant
under
\phi^{t}
the flow
\phi
is globally defined on
D_{\varepsilon}
and

(i)
holds.
Remark
4.9
implies (
8.8
).
Finally (
8.14
) implies (
8.9
).
Moreover, if
H(\phi^{t_{1}}(v))=H(\phi^{t_{2}}v))
for some
t_{2}>t_{1}
then (
8.9
) implies that
(\nabla_{v}\Phi)(c(v),v)
vanishes on
\phi^{t}(v)
for any
t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]
as well as
Y(v)
in (
8.10
)
(if
(\nabla_{v}\Phi)(c(v),v)=0
then
\mu(v)=0
by (
8.12
) and
Y(v)=0
).
\hfill\square

Multiplicity of
3d
-Stokes waves: proof of Theorem
1.1
.

Let

a
as in
(
8.1
)-(
8.2
).
In view of Lemma

8.5
, stationary points in
\mathbb{M}_{a}^{{3d}}
of the gradient-like flow

\phi^{t}(\cdot)
of
H
restricted to
\mathbb{M}_{a}
give rise to truly
3d
Stokes waves.
If there are infinitely many stationary

{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-orbits of the gradient-like flow, then there exist also infinitely many

{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}

critical orbits of
H
, thus geometrically distinct
3d
-Stokes waves.
Otherwise
we apply Theorem
3.14

to the
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-invariant continuous
function

F:\underbrace{M}_{=S(V_{-})\times S(V_{+})}\star\underbrace{S^{1}}_{\equiv S(V_{0})}\to{\mathbb{R}}\,,\quad F:=H\circ\gamma^{-1}\,,

where
\gamma
is the

{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}
-equivariant homeomorphism of Theorem

7.3
-(
\daleth 2
).
By the comments below (
8.3
), the action
(\tau_{\theta})_{\theta\in{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}}
acts transitively
on the orbit
S^{1}
,
and
the stabilizers
at any point
of
M
are finite.
The function
F
has the gradient-like flow
\gamma\circ\phi^{t}_{|\mathbb{M}_{a}}\circ\gamma^{-1}
for which
(cf. Remark
8.1
)
all
S^{1}
is stationary.
Therefore Theorem
3.14

proves the existence
of at least
\mathrm{CL}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}}(M)+1

critical values
of
F

different
from the critical level

\ell_{*}=F(S^{1})
,
and thus
the same number of

{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
-critical orbits of
H

at a level different from

\ell_{*}=H(\mathbb{M}_{a}^{{2d}})
.
In view of (
8.3
) these orbits are contained in
\mathbb{M}_{a}^{{3d}}
, thus are truly
3d
-waves.

Lemma 8.8
.

\mathrm{CL}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}}(S(V_{-})\times S(V_{+}))\geq\tfrac{1}{2}(\dim(V_{-})+\dim(V_{+}))-{2}=\#{\mathcal{V}}-{3}
.

Proof.

The lower bound for
\mathrm{CL}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{2}_{\Gamma}}(M)

is provided by Example
3.13

with
n_{1}=\tfrac{1}{2}\dim(V_{-})

and
n_{2}=\tfrac{1}{2}\dim(V_{+})
.
Let us verify the non-collinearity assumption (
2.29
).
The spheres
S(V_{-})
and
S(V_{+})
correspond to
S(k_{1})
and
S(k_{2})
with matrices
k_{l}={\bf A}^{\top}(j_{l,1},\dots,j_{l,n_{l}})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2\times n_{l}}
for
l=1,2
where
{\bf A}
is the matrix defining the lattice
\Gamma
(cf. (
1.17
)) and

(8.16)

{\mathcal{V}}_{-}:=\{j_{1,i}\}_{i=1}^{n_{1}}:=\{j\in{\mathcal{V}}\ |\ j\cdot a^{\bot}<0\}\,,\quad{\mathcal{V}}_{+}:=\{j_{2,i}\}_{i=1}^{n_{2}}=\{j\in{\mathcal{V}}\ |\ j\cdot a^{\bot}>0\}\,.

Any pair of vectors in
{\mathcal{V}}_{-}
and
{\mathcal{V}}_{+}

are independent
(non-collinear)
and thus (
2.29
) holds.
The lemma follows by
(
3.27
) and

\dim(V_{-})+\dim(V_{+})=2\#{\mathcal{V}}-\dim(V_{0})=2(\#{\mathcal{V}}-1)
.
∎

We conclude the existence of at least
\#{\mathcal{V}}-{2}
distinct geometrically
distinct
3d
-Stokes waves.

Non-collinear case: proof of Theorem
1.2
.

In view of Theorem
7.3
-
(\daleth 1)
the set
\mathbb{M}_{a}
is a manifold of
3d
-waves
in
V\setminus{\bf V}^{2d}
,
equivariantly diffeomorphic to
S(V_{-})\times S(V_{+})
, and any stabilizer
({\mathbb{T}}_{\Gamma}^{2})_{v}
,
v\in\mathbb{M}_{a}
, is finite (cf. (
4.26
)).
Then Theorem
1.2

follows similarly by
Theorem
3.15

and
(
3.27
).

Proof of Theorem

1.3
.

Let
r>0
be as Lemma
4.3
,
r^{\prime}\in(0,r)
as in the definition
(
5.42
) of
c(v)
and
\varepsilon>0
be fixed by Theorem
7.3
.
We recall that the set

D_{\varepsilon}

in (
7.7
)
and the range
U_{2}
of the
straightening
homeomorphism
\zeta

(cf. Theorem
6.2
) satisfy

D_{\varepsilon}\subset U_{2}\subset B_{r^{\prime}}^{V}\subset B_{r}^{V}
.
We take
\delta\in(0,{r^{\prime}})

small such that

(8.17)

\Pi_{V}B_{\delta}^{X}\subset D_{\varepsilon}\qquad\text{where}\qquad B_{\delta}^{X}:=\big\{u\in X\ |\ \|u\|_{X}<\delta\big\}

and
\Pi_{V}
is the projector onto
V
associated to the decomposition (
4.11
).
The Lyapunov-Schmidt reduction describes
all
the small amplitude Stokes waves with speed near
c_{*}
.

Lemma 8.9
.

Let

(c,u)\in B_{\delta}(c_{*})\times B_{\delta}^{X}
be
a Stokes wave solution of (
1.14
). Then

u=v+w(c(v),v)
where

v:=\Pi_{V}u\in D_{\varepsilon}
and

c(v)
is defined in (
5.42
).

Proof.

Lemmata
4.3
and
4.4
imply that

(8.18)

u=v+w(c,v)\,,\quad v=\Pi_{V}u\,\,,\quad{\rm d}_{v}\Phi(c,v)=0\,\quad\text{where}\ v\in D_{\varepsilon}

by (
8.17
).
If
